# Orchestration Plan - Directed Fanout Run

LIVING DOC. The director reads this each cycle and picks the next OPEN
session (top-to-bottom, phase order A -> F). The executor cycle updates it:
flip the picked session OPEN -> WIP -> DONE, fill the Commit sha, and append any
newly discovered work to the Findings log at the bottom. When no session is OPEN,
the director emits NO_WORK and the loop self-terminates.

**The director is CLAUDE, not Gemini.** That vendor was retired 2026-08-01 and
the loop has been single-vendor since - director, executor, adjudicator and
auditor are all Claude. This header said "Gemini-Directed" until 2026-08-16
while the operator-directive paragraph further down already recorded the
retirement ("NO gemini director - that vendor is retired (2026-08-01)"), so the
file refuted itself, and `ROADMAP.md` designates this doc "machine-read each
cycle" - making the stale header the first thing a director read.

Per-cycle contract (enforced by ops/loop/director_prompt.md):
orchestrator multi-agent fanout (disjoint-file worktree subagents, sole merger,
verifier-gate each slice before merge) -> TDD (failing test first) -> py_compile
before any restart -> full pytest suite green -> UI sessions also pass the 5-phase
fixture audit (STRUCTURE / TYPOGRAPHY / HIT-TARGETS / ASCII / HIERARCHY) + a
overlay visual check via the Playwright ui_recon harness + :8810 static preview (NOT Claude_Preview vs :8888, cannot attach per R2) -> commit with a descriptive message ->
push to origin/main -> /done ritual (append docs/LEDGER.md, sync ROADMAP + this
file) -> "C:\Users\Administrator\AppData\Local\Programs\Python\Python314\python.exe" ops/loop/done_sentinel.py. No AskUserQuestion; auto-pick safest option.

ASCII only. No em-dashes, en-dashes, or smart quotes.

lives on disk: this table plus docs/LEDGER.md plus the git history.

(REGRESS-first) -> parallel worktree slices on disjoint file sets, one merger ->
TDD failing test first -> read-only verifier subagent per slice, defaulting to
REFUTE when uncertain -> merge only green plus verified -> commit -> push ->
append docs/LEDGER.md and sync ROADMAP plus this table.

**Cycle 4 is the argument for merging into one trunk.** S21's failure was invisible to every
slice individually - S17 could not know the guard's shape, S10 could not know a future test
would gate on LFS content. It appeared only when both landed in the same tree and the whole
suite ran. Five green branch reports would have shipped it.

**What this run is actually evidence for.** Every filed count that was re-derived was wrong -
B5 "about 22" was 1, B4 "about 22" was 11, B2 "19" was 20, and two ROADMAP rows I wrote
myself were refuted by the slices I sent to execute them. The adversarial pass earned its
cost every cycle: it constructed a catastrophic write, caught a skip converted into a
no-op, found a guard pointed at a different address than the thing it guarded, and twice
found a true conclusion resting on a citation that did not exist. **Treat a filed number as
a hypothesis, and treat "the slice says it is green" as unverified until something tried to
break it.**

Deliberately NOT picked, with the reason: RM-122 (operator-present UI/UX - the
row's own note forbids a headless agent picking it), RM-167 and RM-168 and RM-151
and RM-155 arena half (live-game gated), RM-125 rendered residue (routed to
RM-122), RM-164 consumer wiring (coach-surface change, operator-gated).

- 2026-07-28 R225 ds-rm97-pet-damage-probe (gemini-loop cycle 32, head `df08c049`) DONE - **a one-line gap spec was right about the hole and wrong about its shape, and only the enumeration could tell them apart.** RM-97 reads "persistent pet damage scores exactly ZERO". Measured: true of the DAMAGE numerator, false as a blanket claim - `objdamage.py:79-83` (SUMMON_DPS 0.55) and `zonecontrol.py:73-77` (SUMMON 0.60) already credit the identical champion list on two non-damage axes. And the class is not uniform: 7 pet-bearing forms carry ZERO `attribute_kind == "damage"` blocks (Zyra P + W, Heimerdinger Q **both** turret forms, Ivern R, Yorick P, Yorick R) while 3 summon forms DO carry exactly one and are credited (Malzahar W, Annie R burst, Elise W spiderling explosion). The correct sentence is "no PERSISTENT-ENTITY uptime model", not "no summon ever scores" - the second reads as unbuilt, the first names the missing schema. **The near-miss the probe found is the reusable one:** Yorick R's ONLY block is `("Mist Walkers","other")`, a mist-walker COUNT sitting at `damage_blocks[0]`, harmless solely because `_select_blocks` (`ability_dps.py:459`) filters on `attribute_kind` BEFORE taking index 0 - drop that filter and a unit count scores as magic damage. That is now pinned. Also: the P row is ABSENT, not zero (`SPELL_KEYS` excludes "P"), so the pin asserts what was observed rather than what the plan expected. **Process note worth carrying: an engine-impact-NONE, test-only cycle still owes the DS Share sync** - a new file under `agents/daemon_slayer/tests/` is a mirrored source and `test_ds_share_sync_determinism.py` went red with `DRIFT (missing from Share/src)` until `tools/ds_share_sync.py` ran.

Probed live: `current.txt`=16.14.1, but the newest persisted table on disk is 16.13.1 (the full-roster tables are Git-LFS, ~66MB/mode, and are NOT auto-regenerated on a patch bump - [[reference_hz_precompute_patch_regen]]), and `load_laning_scenarios()` resolved the current patch STRICTLY (`use_patch = patch or resolve_patch()`, no descend) so all 3 modes returned `{}` - every current-patch shadow tick accrued nothing, stalling the exact signal that gates flipping Haiku off. The obvious fix (regenerate the 16.14.1 table) is ~118 min single-process + a 200MB LFS commit - too heavy + too mechanical for one cycle, and it recurs EVERY patch bump. Root-cause fix instead: a prior-patch graceful fallback in the read layer - when the caller does not pin an explicit patch and the current-patch file is absent, serve the newest AVAILABLE prior-patch table (`_latest_available_patch`, numeric-version sort so 16.10.1 > 16.9.1 not lexicographic), tagged `_stale_patch`/`_served_patch`/`_requested_patch` so the (DEFAULT-OFF `RC_LANING_CV_SERVED`) live-flip gate can DECLINE stale data (honoring "a wrong precompute is worse than a Haiku call"). An explicit `patch=` pin is honored verbatim (no silent substitute). TDD RED-first `PatchFallbackTests` x6; the self-heal test surfaced a real cache-correctness bug - the mtime-only invalidation could serve a stale fallback after the real table lands because two DIFFERENT files can share a coarse Windows-clock mtime, so the `_CACHE` value became a 3-tuple `(mtime, payload, served)` and the hit-guard now checks served-patch too (invalidates on a fallback->real transition). Additive `_stale_patch` top-level key proven harmless to every consumer (1051-test laning/hz/precompute/deterministic/build_order set green, 0 fail). Live-proven: sr/aram/arena now all serve 16.13.1 (1,795,740 leaves) tagged stale instead of dark. Verifier gate ALL-CONFIRM 7/7 (RED-first plausible, 46 module + 1051 consumer green, 3-tuple cache guard present, live probe stale-tagged, ruff clean, numeric-sort test discriminating). ENGINE-IMPACT NONE (core/ read layer, no `agents/daemon_slayer/` math, no ENGINE_VERSION bump, no Share sync, no DS bounce); Tier-1, RC restart to land live. done_sentinel --tests <N> --regressions 0. Don't-redo: `load_laning_scenarios` now falls back across the post-bump regen gap - do NOT re-pitch "Lane A is dark on a fresh patch"; the fallback is shadow-safe + self-healing. OWED (FUTURE, not built - heavy scheduled regen not a cycle build): the real 16.14.1 full-roster table via `python core/laning_scenario_precompute.py --mode all` (~2h, ~200MB LFS) - the fallback keeps Lane A warm until it lands, and a patch-refresh hook to regen laning_scenarios on bump would close the recurring gap permanently.

Reverted the promotion + the (bulk 125-file) ENGINE bump; refreshed only the note (Tier-0, no math, no bump); Share --check green (1.239.0, 460 files); DS suite 9204 passed. **Lesson, same family as R135/R133: a mirror-parity invariant is not free - "the twin credits its own line" is correct ONLY when both ids belong in the same build pool. - 2026-07-20 R137 (gemini-loop cycle 7, head cb926c2e) DONE (`f7e82ff3`) - **one lesson dominates: a premise can be stale in the direction of work ALREADY DONE, not just work that is wrong.** Half this directive asked for a consolidation `2d003ac5` had already shipped, and the cheap check was a `git log --grep` before dispatching a single build agent. The other half named a fix site (`tests/snapshot_panels/`) that provably contains none of the mechanism - zero asyncio hits - because the leak is third-party. Second lesson: **the repo's own docs manufactured the dead end.** "playwright never clears the marker" was wrong; `__exit__` does clear it, and once that is true the bug is a fixture-SCOPE tradeoff rather than an unfixable third-party defect. A wrong mechanism note is worse than a missing one - it forecloses the real option. Third: **two of the three guard defects were found by adversarial probing, not by reading.** The verifier surfaced the root-conftest-only scan gap, and the orchestrator's own RED probe failed for the wrong reason (a BOM `SyntaxError`), which is the only way the utf-8 vs utf-8-sig crash would have been found - a guard that goes red for the wrong reason is indistinguishable from one that works, so always read WHY it went red, never just THAT it did.
- 2026-07-19 R136-audit (gemini-loop cycle 6, head db08c374) DONE - **the directed fix was a no-op on a phantom regression, and the real defect was in the instrument that reported it.** The cycle-5 audit returned REGRESS on three claims: that R136 edited the read-only mirror `Share/src/agents/daemon_slayer` directly, that the true source `agents/daemon_slayer` was "missing from diff", and that ENGINE bumped without tests. All three are false. `git show --name-only 18aca9e3` counts 275 files: 139 under `Share/` and **129 under `agents/daemon_slayer/`**, including every file the directive ordered "moved" (`_rune_health_grants.py`, `_rune_hsp_amp.py`, `ehp.py`, `hybrid.py`, `server.py`) plus three NEW R136 tests. HEAD was provably healthy at audit time: `ds_share_sync.py --check` exit 0 (engine 1.225.0, 480 files), the R136 tests 73 passed / 435 subtests, and both `ci` and `CodSpeed` green on db08c374. Executing the directive would have been actively destructive - Agent 1's "revert manual Share/ edits" deletes mirror content that the currently-green `ds_share_sync --check` CI guard requires, converting a clean tree into a red one. **Root cause, and the durable half: the auditor was fed a head-truncated diff.** `auditor()` spent its entire 55,000-byte budget on a raw `git diff`, and git emits paths in byte order, so `Share/` (0x53) precedes `agents/` (0x61); on a commit that touches a generated mirror AND its true source, the mirror padding consumes the budget and the true source never reaches the prompt. The auditor then cannot distinguish "absent from the commit" from "past the cut" and resolves the ambiguity as a regression. Fix rebudgets the payload rather than growing it, because `GEMINI_STDIN_CAP = 60_000` is an empirically-measured cliff (silent EMPTY stdout above it) and must not be crossed: `AUDIT_DIFF_CAP = 40_000` yields 15K to a complete `--name-status` manifest (`AUDIT_MANIFEST_CAP = 12_000`) emitted BEFORE the body, carrying a total file count so manifest truncation is self-evident, and the body's cut notice now states that later-sorting paths are dropped first and that absence below is not evidence of absence. Replayed against the real commits the payload is 54,782 bytes (under cap) and names all 280 changed files. **Lesson, third cycle running in this same family: an audit verdict is a claim, not evidence, and it earns the same premise-check as a feature claim.** Cycle 3's auditor inferred a callee's semantics from an argument name; this one inferred a commit's contents from a truncated view of it. The sharper generalization is about tooling rather than reasoning: **when a tool truncates its input, absence of evidence silently becomes evidence of absence,** and any consumer downstream will confidently report the artifact of the cut as a finding. The guard is `test_truncated_diff_still_lists_late_sorting_true_source` in `tests/test_loop_audit_range.py`, which builds the exact mirror-before-source topology and fails if a changed path past the cut is not named in the prompt. Note the running controller had already loaded the old module, so the fix takes effect at the next `launch_loop.ps1`, not mid-run.
- 2026-07-19 R135 (gemini-loop cycle 4, head 20532b3e) DONE - **the sweep's own thesis was refuted by measurement, and the shippable defect was the thing found on the way past it.** The directive ordered a movespeed soft-cap seam and pre-declared ENGINE-IMPACT BUMP. Both lanes came back CLEAN on that thesis, and the reason matters more than the verdict: the soft cap is not merely unimplemented, it is UNREACHABLE at every site. Roster base MS spans 315-355 so the 220/415/490 breakpoints cannot bind on the `est_ms` path, and the DS path that CAN exceed 415 is double-gated behind a DEFAULT-OFF flag with no production caller AND an engine credit cap (`_MS_UTILITY_DPS_CAP = 0.15`) that already absorbs the error to under 0.8 percentage points. **A missing guard is not a bug until something can reach it - measure reachability before pricing the fix.** Had the directive's BUMP been taken at face value, this cycle would have shipped a DEFAULT-OFF seam, an ENGINE_VERSION bump, a Share re-sync and a `:8860` bounce to correct a quantity that is provably zero on every live path. Second lesson, and the sharper one: **the existing test passed because it tested the one case outside the failing class.** `test_champion_movespeed.py` pinned `item_ms("Boots of Swiftness")`, and Boots of Swiftness is precisely the shape that cannot exhibit the bug - a canonical id that HAS movespeed and therefore already claims its own name key. Every item that grants NONE was silently answered by its Arena/ARAM mirror. A green suite over a sampled-wrong fixture is indistinguishable from a green suite over a correct one, so when a lane reports CLEAN, check what the tests actually sample before believing the code is exercised. This is the same family as R133's `test_arena_mirrors_credit_same_as_base` pinning the defect as an invariant, one rung down: R133 asserted the wrong thing, R135 asserted the right thing about the wrong row. Third, procedural and cheap: the directive's `ENGINE-IMPACT` header is a PREDICTION, not a fact, and the precommit gate is a free independent check on it - "no mirrored DS source staged" confirmed the no-bump call without a judgement call. Fourth: `items_meraki.json` has NO `stats` key on any of its 320 entries, so DDragon is the sole MS magnitude source and the usual "cross-check against Meraki bulk" habit silently cannot run here - worth knowing before the next registry sweep assumes it did. Durable hazard pinned for future audits: `"22" + base_id` is NOT a mirror-pairing rule - `3172` is Gunmetal Greaves while `223172`/`663172` are Zephyr, and Meraki still calls `3172` "Zephyr" while DDragon (authoritative) does not.
- 2026-07-19 R134 (gemini-loop cycle 3, head 308b3440) DONE - the REGRESS audit was 2/3 right, and the wrong third was the one that would have shipped a real bug. The directive ordered a `total_armor` "math fix" in `ehp.py`: it read the call `_rune_resist_fn(..., total_armor=armor)`, saw a separate local named `bonus_armor`, concluded item armor was missing, and prescribed `total_armor=armor+bonus_armor+ext_armor+item_resist_armor`. Every term is misidentified. `armor` is `stats["armor"]`, the RESOLVED build armor, which already contains item armor; `bonus_armor` at ehp.py:1668 is `resist_grants(...)` OUTPUT - the CHAMPION registry, a peer conditional-grant lane, not a stat; `item_resist_armor` is the ITEM registry output, itself derived from `total_armor=armor`; `ext_armor` is an ALLY-conferred resist. Applying it feeds three peer grant lanes into the fourth, so Aftershock takes 75 percent of OTHER grants, and the result becomes dependent on the SOURCE ORDER of four peer registries - the last one evaluated is the only one that sees the others. `_rune_resist_grants.rune_resist_grants:227-230` states the contract verbatim ("the same values the champion `resist_grants` and the item `item_resist_grants` receive") and the call site already honored it. **The lesson is the exact inverse of cycle 2's and the same failure family: cycle 2's agent paraphrased a formula it had cited correctly; cycle 3's auditor inferred a callee's semantics from an argument NAME at the call site without reading the callee's own contract.** Both are "cited the right line, got the claim wrong". Verifying a REGRESS verdict deserves the same premise-check as verifying a feature claim - an audit is not privileged evidence. Second lesson: **reclassify before you remediate.** Regressions 2 and 3 (mid-signature kwarg inserts in `rank_items_by_ehp` / `compute_hybrid` / `rank_items_by_hybrid`) were real but were latent CONVENTION drift, not functional breakage - the suite was green at 20659 precisely because nothing passes positionally 25 parameters deep. Right-sizing them collapsed a directed 2-worktree ENGINE-bump slice into a 6-line reorder with no bump. Third, the durable part: `compute_ehp` had carried the convention as an inline COMMENT ("Appended at END ..."), and three siblings violated it anyway. The fix ships `test_rune_resist_signature_convention_r134.py`, which enforces the trailing-kwarg convention AND pins the `total_armor` same-values contract, so the refuted change now turns a test RED instead of silently over-crediting the tank cohort the feed exists for. A convention that lives only in a comment is not a convention.
- 2026-07-19 R132+R133 (gemini-loop cycle 2, head 3d5458a9) DONE - one lesson dominates. **Verifying an agent's CITATIONS is not the same as verifying its CLAIM.** The rune sweep agent's report was excellent: every file:line it cited checked out under an independent grep, its REFUTEs were correct, and it correctly identified a real structural hole. It still got the central formula wrong. It reported Aftershock as `45 + 75% of Bonus Resists` and omitted the trailing longDesc clause "capped at: 80-150 (based on level)". Had the orchestrator merged on citation-checking alone, R132 would have shipped math that over-credits every high-resist tank - the exact cohort the feature exists to serve - because the cap binds at 150 while the uncapped value reaches 157.5 by level 18. What caught it was re-reading the RAW `runesReforged.json` longDesc rather than the agent's paraphrase of it. Generalization: when a finding hands you a FORMULA, reproduce the formula from source before building, not just the file:line that mentions it. This sits alongside the existing probe-trap memories - same failure family, new surface (paraphrase drift rather than probe-parameter drift). Second lesson, cheaper but sharp: **a suite can pin the very defect it should catch.** R133's `test_arena_mirrors_credit_same_as_base` asserted `base == mirror`, which is precisely the "base nominal" copy bug expressed as an invariant - so the wrong magnitudes were not merely uncaught, they were PROTECTED. When fixing a data-seeding bug, always ask what the existing tests assert about the seeded values before assuming a red test means a new break. Third, procedural: the sweep found FOUR gaps where the directive expected one, and the disciplined response was to build ONE (plus one executor-opened correctness fix on disjoint files) and spec the other four read-only as RM-99 / RM-101..RM-104, rather than batch-building on a single cycle's verification budget.
- 2026-07-19 R130+R131 (gemini-loop cycle 1, head 22299147) DONE - two lessons worth keeping. (1) **"Ready" is not health, and exit 0 is not success.** RC-GeminiAudit read Enabled/Ready at every session-start probe while producing nothing for 28 nights, because the failure mode was a dangling resume-marker sha driving the script down its own "nothing to audit" exit-0 branch BEFORE any logging. The anomaly probe surfaced only the unrelated 0xC000013A. Any scheduled job whose success path can be silent needs an unconditional first-write; that is now the fix. Generalizable: a persisted-sha-as-git-range-endpoint is a defect class, and the sweep found `gemini_audit.ps1` was the only instance - every other rev-parse in tools/ops/scripts uses live symbolic refs, and `loop_controller.py:112-116` already validates with `rev-parse --verify -q`. (2) **Two orchestrator hypotheses were REFUTED by their own slice agents, both correctly.** The executor proposed `InteractiveToken` session-teardown as the 0xC000013A cause; the agent killed it with a same-night control (5 sibling InteractiveToken tasks, all result 0, one running one second earlier) and correctly declined to re-register a working task on refuted evidence. The executor framed all 3 CI failures as ordering pollution; the agent proved only 2 were, and that the third was an environmental live-engine dependency CI can never satisfy. Both agents were briefed to verify premises rather than accept them, and both did - keep briefing them that way. Also: an agent hit a `schtasks /NP` password prompt during repro and correctly killed the process rather than supplying a credential. Nightly full-suite had been red 12 consecutive days and no one noticed, because the push `check` job is green by construction and is what everyone looks at.

**R163 - THE WHOLESALE-REWRITE PREMISE WAS REFUTED ON GROUND TRUTH, and one real
defect was found and fixed instead.** The directive framed Share/ as an unworked
artifact needing an end-to-end external-presentation overhaul. It is not: the eight
authored files are already a polished, internally-consistent external package -
`README.md` credits all six upstream sources explicitly (Riot Data Dragon /
CommunityDragon / Meraki / community wiki / lolmath.net + the named-not-shipped
Overlay App E feed) in two places, `LICENSE.md` repeats the per-source terms with the
CC-BY-SA obligation and the Riot non-endorsement disclaimer, all six doc anchors
track live (engine 1.239.0 / patch 16.14.1), `ds_share_sync.py --check` was GREEN
at baseline, and every authored file is ASCII-clean (0 non-ASCII bytes, verified).
A fabricated rewrite of accurate technical docs would have been net-negative and
high-risk against a public product artifact - so it was not done (precedent: the
R114 / R115 CLEAN-REFUTED-PREMISE rows).

**The one genuine defect: `README.md` still described the RETIRED test-packaging
state.** Its At-a-glance row, its entire "What that test run reports" narrative, and
its Notation section said the package ships **345 test files / 9098 tests**, that
running the suite yields **8816 passed / 108 failed / 16 skipped / 158 errors** with
**46 of the 345 files reaching outside**, and that a reviewer must build a **46-line
`--ignore` block** to reach a green baseline (measured **6920 passed**). That state
was retired by RM-112 (2026-07-23): the 49 host-coupled modules are now excluded from
the mirror at generation, so the shipped package is **306 of the engine's 355 source
test files** and the plain command exits GREEN. `docs/05_AUDIT_AND_REFACTOR.md` had
already been updated to that reality; README had not, so it contradicted its own
audit doc and told an external reviewer to expect 108 failures and 158 errors that
cannot occur. FIX: reconciled README's three stale sites to the re-measured current
truth - I ran the shipped suite myself (`PYTHONPATH=. python -m pytest
agents/daemon_slayer/tests -q` from `Share/src`) and observed **7270 passed, 16
skipped, 2330 subtests, 0 failed, 0 errors, 66.7s, exit 0**, matching doc 05 exactly.

**Verification.** `ds_share_sync.py --check` GREEN post-edit (anchors intact, `src`
mirror byte-identical - README/LICENSE/docs are authored, not part of the
deterministic mirror). Share doc-guard + hygiene suite (`test_ds_share_*` x11 +
smart-quote / mojibake / u2500) **110 passed + 24 subtests**. README **0 non-ASCII
bytes**. No test pins the old README numbers (grep-checked). Inline single-file edit
(premise collapsed the 4-slice fan-out to one file; R7 exempts an own single-thread
edit from the verifier-subagent gate, and every claim was independently re-measured
against ground truth). Don't-redo: Share/ is already a complete, credited, ASCII-clean
external artifact - do NOT re-pitch a wholesale rewrite; the README test-suite numbers
are now the RM-112 306/355/49 + 7270-green reality and track doc 05.

SHIPPED by R145 in `_rune_offense_grants.py`. 8138 Eyeball Collection, 8136 Zombie Ward
and 8120 Ghost Poro are NOT PRESENT in `data/meta_build/ddragon/16.14.1/runesReforged.json`
at all - they were removed from the game, so they are dead ids, not gaps. 8210
Transcendence grants Ability Haste only, and the AH axis is MEASURED INERT and settled.
Only 8010 Conqueror was genuinely uncredited on the self side. Two further directive

(`data/daemon_slayer/16.14.1/runesReforged.json`) does not exist, and the NEW module +

seam. Scope was redirected to extending the existing registry.

**AN ADVERSARIAL SLICE REFUTED THE ORCHESTRATOR'S OWN BRIEF MID-RUN, and that is the
headline.** The build agent was originally instructed to mirror `enemy_runes.py:149` at
21.6 -> 48.0. That is correct ADAPTIVE FORCE but it is NOT an AD number: the enemy lane
uses AF as a raw damage proxy and never as a stat, so it converts nothing. Riot's rule is
1 AF = 1 AP or 0.6 AD, so the uncorrected brief would have inflated the AD column by
1/0.6 = 1.667x. The refutation was issued before the builder finished and the correction
was applied in-slice. The 0.6 ratio is pinned by a property test against the registry's
OWN sibling rows (`round(0.6 * ap) == ad` on all 7 stated values), not by an asserted
constant. Two further corrections landed from the same refutation: the stack count became
a knob (`_ASSUMED_CONQUEROR_STACKS` = 12.0 + per-call `conqueror_stacks`, clamped 0-12)
because `rune_procs.py:212` puts the stack count on the caller, and the docstring stopped
citing the enemy lane as precedent because max-stacks INVERTS from conservative
(enemy-threat lens) to optimistic (self lens).

VERIFIER GATE (read-only, 9 claims, pre-merge): MERGE-SAFE YES. It independently
reproduced the DEFAULT-OFF byte-identity (Jinx L18 / 3031,3094 / armor 100 =
`65.28245833333332` with and without `rune_ids=['8010']`, exact equality; flag ON gives
`73.18805833333334`, so the seam is non-vacuous), confirmed the stack knob actually
reaches the grant, and diffed the two pre-existing tests to confirm neither assertion was
gutted to pass.

**R152 - PENETRATION IS REGISTRY-ONLY, AND THAT IS THE WHOLE FINDING.** Two disjoint
read-only sweep agents (lethality/%armorpen and flat/%magicpen) converged independently
on the same crux: `agents/daemon_slayer/stats.py` `ITEM_STAT_KEY_MAP` has no penetration
key, and DDragon's machine-readable `stats` block never carries one - the union across all
706 items is 12 keys, none of them pen. Penetration lives ONLY in the `<stats>` HTML of the
`description` string. The local `items_meraki.json` has NO top-level stat block on any of
its 320 items. So `ITEM_EFFECTS` is the sole credit path and any missing `lethality=` field
is a silent 0.0. Do NOT go looking for a generic stat-block path; it does not exist.

**THE ORCHESTRATOR'S OWN 706-ITEM SWEEP BEAT BOTH AGENTS' RANKED LISTS.** Both agents
returned a "highest-value one-line fix" naming a single item. A direct parity sweep
(26 exact-match / 12 divergent) showed the 12 were TWO root causes, not one ranked list:
RC-A (stat entirely absent, 5 ids) and RC-B (Arena magnitude drift, 7 ids). Only RC-A is a
data bug. Shipping one agent's top pick would have left the other four RC-A ids uncredited
and, worse, invited a later slice to "fix" RC-B by weakening a guard.

**RESOLVED R161 - RC-B ARENA STAT-LINE DRIFT, DECIDED AS DOCTRINE B (director call, shipped
with ENGINE 1.237.0 -> 1.238.0).** THE DECISION: when a DDragon Arena mirror states its OWN
explicit stat line and that line differs from its Summoner's Rift twin, THE ARENA FEED VALUE
WINS. Arena mirrors no longer inherit SR base stat magnitudes. Inheritance of passive
COEFFICIENTS where Meraki has no mirror entry is UNCHANGED - that is the case the rule was
written for, and only explicitly-stated base stat lines flip. The rejected alternative,
doctrine A ("mirrors inherit everything from the SR twin"), is DEAD: do not present it as
the standing rule and do not re-file the question as open.

TWELVE ROWS ARE RE-CREDITED FROM THEIR OWN FEED ENTRY. Lethality: `223142` Youmuu's 18 -> 22,
`223814` Edge of Night 15 -> 14, `224004` Spectral Cutlass 15 -> 21, `226676` The Collector
10 -> 12, `226699` Voltaic Cyclosword 10 -> 20, `226701` Opportunity 18 -> 15, `226691`
Duskblade 18 -> 22 (unbuyable on every map and therefore inert, re-credited for consistency
rather than for effect). Flat magic pen: `223020` Sorcerer's Shoes 12 -> 20, `224645`
Shadowflame 15 -> 10. Percent armor pen: `223036` Lord Dominik's 0.35 -> 0.40, `226694`
Serylda's 0.35 -> 0.40. And `223302` Terminus 0.30 -> 0.24 on BOTH pen axes, because the
Arena feed states 8 percent per stack against the SR 10 at the same cap of 3.

HIGHEST LIVE IMPACT IS THE BOOT, not the lethality list. `223020` is the Arena mage DEFAULT
boot, so it lands in essentially every Arena AP build, and at 12 instead of 20 it was
under-crediting magic damage by roughly 5.7-6.9 percent through `effective_target_mr`.

ROOT CAUSE: Meraki carries no mirror entry for these ids, so the Arena mirrors were seeded
"same as SR" and DDragon was never re-read per-mirror. CORRECTED IN R161 - the older
shorthand "Meraki carries no 22xxxx/44xxxx mirror ids at all" is FALSE and should not be
repeated: the pinned 16.13.1 snapshot carries 17 of them (`220000`-`220007`, `223005`,
`223039`, `223069`, `223185`, `224004`, `224403`, `226630`, `228001`, `228002`). It simply
does not carry `223302`, which is the only claim the Terminus guard ever needed. What kept
this open across R152, R153 and R160 is that the inheritance had guard tests behind it
(`test_arena_prowlers_lethality_same_as_sr`, `test_arena_serylda_armor_pen_diverges_from_sr`
- inverted and renamed in R161 - and `test_terminus_juxtaposition_r67.py:118`, which
STRUCTURALLY derived the Arena value from the SR Meraki entry and asserted it on both ids
off the pinned 16.13.1 Meraki file, not 16.14.1). A guard asserting an inheritance the feed
contradicts protects the defect instead
of the invariant - which is why three sweeps could correctly REPORT the drift and none of
them could decide it. Doctrine B is also not novel to the registry, it is now consistent
with it: `226695` Arena Serpent's Fang has always credited its own feed value of 19 against
the SR `6695` 15.

**OPEN - smaller tails.** `1111` Jarvan I's carries 12 flat magic pen and has NO
`ITEM_EFFECTS` entry at all, though DS does register its 10 AH (`_item_ability_haste.py:50`)
and 30 tenacity (`_item_tenacity.py:61`) - ARAM-only, augment-gated, lowest frequency but
inconsistent with DS's own registration pattern for that same item. `Share/README.md`
release list is stale at 1.228.0 and needs a 1.229.0-1.233.0 backfill (R152 added only its
own 1.234.0 row). `443064` Talisman of Ascension ships literal `?` placeholders in the
snapshot and is genuinely unmodelable - leave it `defensive_only`.

Don't-redo: RC-A is CLOSED (31 exact-match, 0 absent). Penetration has no generic path -
do not re-search for one and do not re-derive magnitudes from `items_meraki.json`. `226695`
Arena Serpent's Fang is 19 where SR `6695` is 15 and that divergence is REAL in the feed -
do not normalize them. `6632`/`226632` Divine Sunderer 3 percent armor pen is uncredited but
INERT (all maps false, unbuyable everywhere) - not a gap. RC-B is CLOSED under doctrine B
(R161): an Arena mirror's own explicit stat line WINS over the SR twin's, so do not re-open
the inheritance question and do not re-pitch doctrine A. `226693` Prowler's Claw is NOT a
divergence and never was - the Arena feed and SR `6693` BOTH state 22 lethality, an exact
match. It was named as a divergent row in the R161 directive and REFUTED during
verification, so its guard test was correctly left un-inverted; do not "correct" it in
either direction.

| R190 | ds-sweep-penetration | DIRECTOR REFILL (rotation source 1: DS sweep). Sweep Lethality, percent Armor Penetration, flat Magic Penetration and percent Magic Penetration magnitudes + Arena/ARAM mirrors vs Meraki / DDragon truth, and land ONE new DEFAULT-OFF math lane on the penetration axis. The real defect is a MISSING SOURCE, not a wrong number: both pipelines accept `ItemEffect` objects ONLY, so CHAMPION-KIT penetration never entered the damage math at all - it was scored solely on the standalone opt-in anti-tank ranking axis (`antitank._ANTITANK_REGISTRY`, the R93 Darius row), which feeds no resist computation. NEW `_kit_penetration.py` closes it with a registry derived row-by-row from `champion_abilities.json` 16.14.1 (Darius E 40 pct, Gangplank E 40 pct keg-conditional, Nilah Q 33 pct crit-scaled, Ambessa R 30 pct, Pantheon R 30 pct - all max-rank, each carrying its verbatim tooltip) plus a pure `apply_kit_penetration(..., enabled=False)` seam; `effective_target_armor` gains `kit_pen_pct` / `kit_pen_flat` APPENDED at the end of the signature with 0.0 defaults, and the kit fraction joins the SAME `_composed_keep_factor` product as item percent pen so the two can never sum (LDR 0.35 with Darius 0.40 is 0.61 effective, not 0.75). Zero production call sites pass either argument - `dps.py:841`, `ability_dps.py:1099`, `burst.py:795` are byte-identical and the live flip stays operator-gated. MAGIC side is a REFUTE made MACHINE-ENFORCED: 14 kit-intrinsic magic-side grants exist, 13 already credited, the lone hole being Annie R (15/17.5/20 pct magic pen, absent from the anti-tank registry entirely) now pinned as known-uncredited so a later crediting slice flips the guard RED. Both guards re-derive their swept set from the shipped ability data every run. MEASURED + CORRECTED: Meraki `items_meraki.json` DOES carry 921 penetration-family blocks under `passives[*].stats` across 214 items, and all 5526 numbers in them are 0.0 - Meraki is NOT a penetration truth source, which is precisely why `ITEM_EFFECTS` stays the sole item credit path. **R189 - A BANNED GLYPH SHIPPED PUBLICLY BECAUSE EVERY ASCII GUARD SCANS BYTES.** The
headline find is not a doc number, it is a hole in the enforcement of the repo's own ASCII
rule. `data/daemon_slayer/spell_cast_rates.json` - mirrored verbatim into the public
`Share/` package as one of the two patch-independent cast-rate tables - carried an em-dash
(U+2014) and a multiplication sign (U+00D7) in its authored `note` field, stored as JSON
`\uXXXX` escapes. Every existing enforcement mechanism reads BYTES
(`test_smart_quote_hygiene`, `test_mojibake_hygiene`, `test_u2500_hygiene`, and the s244
`tools/strip_em_dashes.py` retro-purge), and escaped form is 7-bit clean on disk, so the
file passed all of them while the string a consumer actually decodes still carried both
glyphs. That is why the 2026-05-18 repo-wide purge missed it. Root cause was two-layered
and BOTH layers were fixed, per the CLAUDE.md "Data Fixes" rule that a guard which only
prevents future occurrences leaves the existing bad rows wrong: (1) the generator
`scripts/build_spell_cast_rates.py` was only HALF-fixed by an earlier pass - line 178 had
been converted to ASCII `" - "` but line 179 still emitted a raw U+00D7 multiplication
sign, making the
generator a live re-infection source (and itself the only non-ASCII authored .py in the
tree, 2 bytes); (2) the on-disk artifact predated even the em-dash half of that fix, so it
carried both glyphs and would not have self-healed. Fixed the generator, then BACKFILLED
the artifact by surgical raw-text replacement rather than a JSON round-trip - asserted
exactly one occurrence of each escape, then asserted every non-`note` key byte-identical
before writing - so the 112KB of measured cast-rate floats are provably untouched (112006
-> 111996 bytes, delta exactly the 10 bytes of escape shrinkage). Mirror propagated via
`ds_share_sync.py`. TDD RED-first: new `tests/test_json_authored_glyph_hygiene.py` (4
tests) went RED 3-of-4 pre-fix and GREEN 4/4 after; it closes the CLASS, not the instance,
by json-DECODING then checking the authored metadata keys (`note` / `notes` /
`generated_note` / `source`), deliberately excluding `description` because upstream Data
Dragon item and ability text legitimately carries non-ASCII and the rule governs AUTHORED
content only. Sibling sweep measured the whole population before fixing: 141 DS JSON files,
42 authored-metadata strings, exactly 2 offenders (the file and its mirror) - so the fix is
provably complete, not narrow. **Don't-redo: authored JSON metadata is now guarded at the
DECODED level; do NOT re-pitch a byte-level ASCII sweep for this class - byte scans
structurally cannot see it.**

**R189 - THE SHARE PACKAGE WAS ACCURATE PROSE OVER DRIFTED NUMBERS.** Five disjoint-file

verdict was explicitly a valid outcome) audited the package as a public product artifact.
Every countable claim was re-measured; the narrative needed almost nothing, the counts
needed a lot. R166 had already done the presentation pass, so this second pass measured
only what accrued SINCE - and the accrual was real: shipped test files 306 -> **309**,
source test files 355 -> **358** (the 49-file host-coupled boundary set still reconciles
exactly), engine modules 105 -> **106**, shipped-suite result 7270/2330 -> **7304 passed,
16 skipped, 2393 subtests, 70s** and source-repo 9203 -> **9238 passed, 1 skipped, 3928
subtests** (both measured fresh this cycle, not carried forward). Two claims were not
merely stale but self-contradictory: README advertised **9** core compute functions while
`01_OVERVIEW.md`, the document it points the reader at, tabulates **11**; and
`05_AUDIT_AND_REFACTOR.md` s3.4 closed with a present-tense "the remaining failures
surface..." paragraph directly contradicting s1's `0 failed` on the same page - a reviewer
reading in the documented order would have hit the contradiction inside two files. Slice B
scripted a FULL citation sweep instead of the 25-citation sample it was asked for: **320
`file:line` citations checked, 0 stale symbols**, max line drift 14 - so the docs' own
"the symbol is the reliable key" caveat is honest and no citation needed rewriting. Its
five content fixes were all engine drift the docs had not tracked: the HTTP boolean flag

cycle-chain), unique-passive families **7 -> 9** (`last_whisper` + `void_pen`, ~33 minors
of drift since ENGINE 1.207), `ItemEffect` "~45 defaulted fields" -> **56 fields / 54

at `server.py:539/655/775/890`; only the `apply_rune_offense_grants` GATE is unparsed - the
consequence was right, the claim was wrong). Slice D proved the CHANGELOG's 113-entry

2026-06-03 (leaving the file internally impossible - a newer version dated earlier than its
successor), and 1.76.0 dated 2026-06-01 under a June heading though `cb92d2d9` was authored
2026-05-31; it also normalized 6 condensed bullets from a bare single version to the
`X -> Y` form the file's own stated convention requires, reaching 89/89 uniform. LICENSE

/ Meraki in every sub-package that redistributes their data, incl. `lolmath_ingest` (which

`http://127.0.0.1:8860`. Verifier gate re-probed 16 numeric claims independently:

were **0 commits ahead of main** - the agents left their work as uncommitted working-tree
changes inside `.claude/worktrees/agent-*`, so the standard `git merge --no-ff
origin/<branch>` would have been a silent no-op that merged NOTHING while reporting success,
and `git show <branch>:<path>` reads main's content and would have silently REFUTED every
doc claim. Merged by verified file copy from the disjoint sets instead. **Don't-redo: the

`_burst_off_axis.py` DEFAULT-OFF seam has flag-matrix + route-table coverage but no
dedicated `###` section like its `kit_conversion.py` / `survivability_credit.py` siblings).**

**R169 - THE HASTE AXIS IS COMPLETE; ULTIMATE HASTE IS NOT AN ITEM STAT.** The directive's
[UNVERIFIED] premise had two parts: (a) Ability/Ultimate/Item Haste magnitudes + Arena/ARAM mirrors
match DDragon 16.14.1, and (b) DS scorers parse Ultimate Haste distinct from generic Ability Haste.
Two parallel read-only truth-sweep agents + a verifier gate resolved both. (a) VERIFIED-TRUE: the
canonical guard `ops/audit/item_ah_drift_check.py` reports IN SYNC (220 AH items, added=removed=changed=0,
exit 0); every `_item_ability_haste.py` magnitude matches its OWN DDragon <stats> line; all 89 Arena
(22xxxx) / ARAM (32xxxx) mode mirrors are present and doctrine-B correct (own-line, e.g. 3158 base 10 ->
Arena 223158=40, 3110 base 20 -> ARAM 323110=25). Item Haste is legitimately unmodeled - it is not a
live item stat this patch (0 DDragon items carry the phrase), so there is nothing to model. (b)
FALSE-BUT-BY-DESIGN: Ultimate Haste is NOT a structured item stat (no `ultimateHaste` key in
items_meraki for 3118/3073/3119; it lives only in passive effect TEXT - Malignance Scorn "20 ultimate
haste", Hexplate Hexcharged "30 ultimate haste"). Like the live game, items grant generic Ability Haste;
Ultimate Haste is a rune/boot concept. So DS correctly has NO distinct ult-haste code path (the sole
grep hit is a prose docstring at `_item_ally_grant.py:39`); the real on-ult effects are modeled as
separate ItemEffect procs (Malignance Hatefog magic damage `_effects_data.py:1943-1964`; Hexplate
Overdrive AS/MS burst `:1801-1810`, 30 ult haste pinned "stat-side only"; Winter's Approach "no DPS
proc"). DIRECTIVE ID-ERROR corrected (data, not a defect): the directive named "Experimental Hexplate
3119" but 3119=Winter's Approach (grants zero ult haste) and Experimental Hexplate=3073 (carries no
generic AH, only 40 AD / 20% AS / 450 HP). Per the DECIDE branch NO seam was scaffolded and NO ENGINE
bump taken - the axis is parity-clean, there is no distinct ult-haste stat to parse, and an
ability-haste DPS term is separately CLOSED (ANSWERED-NO 2026-07-18). Verifier ALL-CONFIRM; AH guard

RM-410. **The directive's headline premise was REFUTED before any code was written, by the verifier and then by an independent re-probe.** It asserted "NO test anywhere calls `lib/ddragon/fetch.py`'s own writer" and that the RM-406 class guard "asserts the TARGET files' git attributes and sweeps tracked bytes" only. False: `tests/test_tracked_json_producers_emit_lf_bytes.py` has a BEHAVIOURAL half - `_invoke` at `:115` calls `fn(target, _PAYLOAD)`, `:137` reads `raw = out.read_bytes()`, `:141` asserts `pairs == 0`, `:146` asserts `b"\r" not in raw`, parametrised over all six `_PRODUCERS` rows; `-k fetch` -> 3 passed, 17 deselected. ### Findings log - R229 (2026-09-11)

- **A doc sentence can self-stale inside the very commit that falsifies it, and a director will then re-derive it as a fresh premise months later.** `c00b9af89` added `tests/test_tracked_json_producers_emit_lf_bytes.py` (+229 lines, a behavioural guard that CALLS all six producers) and, in the same diff, the `ORCHESTRATION_PLAN.md` sentence saying the fetch writer is "exercised by no test of its own ... carried in the new class guard's table and nowhere else". The author read a `_PRODUCERS` table row as inert bookkeeping. Two cycles later the director sourced R229's headline premise straight out of that tail and wrote a directive whose `NOT-A-DUPLICATE-OF` block asserted the opposite of the tree. The fix that matters is not the test - it is striking the sentence, which was done in the same commit as the test. **Transferable:** when a commit adds a guard AND a prose claim about coverage, the prose is the half nothing checks.
- **A "prove it RED first" instruction can be unsatisfiable, and complying with it would mean lying.** RM-410's directive ordered a red-proof against the historical pre-fix writer line. That line used compact separators, and `json.dumps(obj, separators=(",", ":"))` emits no literal newline for ANY payload (newlines inside strings escape to backslash-n), so `write_text` and `write_bytes` produce identical bytes even on Windows. The revert is unfailable by construction. The honest substitute is a POSITIVE CONTROL on the REACHABLE regression shape (`indent=2` through `write_text`, measured 20 CRLF pairs against 0), plus a test pinning the no-literal-LF fact so the reasoning is code rather than prose. **Transferable:** before promising a red-proof, check whether the pre-fix shape can fail at all for the payload class in question.
- **A worktree slice's `file:line` citations are pinned to ITS head, not the merge target.** Slice 2 measured at `8ad4b6678` while main was `ca6f42554`; one intervening commit inserted a single line into `ORCHESTRATION_PLAN.md` (924 -> 925) and silently moved two cited ranges by +1. The quoted TEXT was byte-identical, so nothing looked wrong. **Transferable:** re-resolve every citation against the merge target before merging a worktree slice, especially into append-heavy docs.
- **Still open, filed not fixed:** four of the six producers are PARTIAL. `tools/daemon_slayer_extract._atomic_write_json` / `._atomic_write_text` and `tools/daemon_slayer_abilities_extract._atomic_write_json` have CRLF coverage only and no dedicated test; `core/polled_json.atomic_write_json` has direct tests that round-trip through `read_text` and pin no bytes, and its exact-encoding shape (`indent=2, ensure_ascii=False`) is asserted nowhere. Each wants the same five properties row 2 now has.

### Findings log - R227 (2026-09-11)

- **The WRAP cycle itself introduced a regression, and it was a text MOVE - the one defect shape no repo guard in this tree can see.** Fixed in `54fcf67c8` (cycle 72). The RM-407 wrap cut the `**Stated limits, not buried:**` census-guard sentence out of the RM-406 ROADMAP row and pasted it onto the RM-407 row, so RM-407 shipped four residuals that are not true of it while RM-406 - which has no `BACKLOG.md` body row, so its ROADMAP line is its only body - lost its limits entirely. Every byte stayed valid prose; only the OWNER was wrong, which is why ASCII hygiene, link checks, the disposition-drift guard and the RM-id registry all stayed green over it. The audit returned REGRESS; no automated gate did. **The transferable half is the counting trap, not the move:** the same wrap also duplicated `Next free id = RM-410` TWICE ON ONE LINE, and `grep -c` returns `1` both before and after the repair because it counts LINES - only `grep -o` piped to `wc -l` (2 -> 1) can see it. A wrap that verifies its own row edits with `grep -c` is verifying nothing. Population of the misattribution class measured and shown at 2 (`ROADMAP.md:48` FIXED, `ROADMAP.md:49` CORRECT and left alone, `BACKLOG.md` 0), and the move was proved byte-identical against `f6eef7e69` by sha256 rather than eyeballed.
- **The directive's own enumeration command manufactures a phantom.** `git ls-files "*test_*.py"` returns a sixteenth directory, bare `tools`, whose only match is `tools/pytest_guard.py` - the substring "test_" inside "pytest_". A guard built on that predicate files an orphan tree it can never close. The precise universe is 15 trees; 13 are covered by the `tests/` and `agents/daemon_slayer/tests/` prefixes and exactly 2 were orphans. No unexpected third tree exists.
- **A single green run is not a green tree.** The audit slice reported "0 failed" from one run; three runs found a 1-in-3 flake. Any future WIRE verdict on a tree must come from repeated runs under load, not one sample.
- **RM-406's census has a structural blind spot that its own AFTER figure cannot reveal.** State leaving over a SOCKET touches none of its four patched write routes and sits under none of its watched paths. `:8890` is OPEN on Legion, so the 6 `live_supervisor` tests do not skip here and POST real records into the running supervisor. Both slice agents and the verifier's own measurement tripped it. Filed RM-409.
- **The push half is closed, the nightly half is not.** Both wired steps landed in `check` only; `nightly-full-suite` still names neither tree. The guard discloses this as its own limitation rather than hiding it.
- **RM-408 - an instrument with no planted control cannot distinguish a clean tree from a dead probe.** `tools/live_write_tracer.py` carries no positive control, so a run whose patches intercept NOTHING reports a clean zero that reads exactly like a clean tree, and LEDGER 1392's headline AFTER figure rests entirely on that uncontrolled instrument. The sibling repo's equivalent control returned `proved: false` on its FIRST run against a tracer that was working correctly, because Windows `tempfile.mkdtemp` hands back the 8.3 SHORT path while the watcher resolves the LONG form - so the failure mode is real and has already been observed once. Filed as RM-408, not fixed here.
- **A cycle that measures and adjudicates but never commits leaves HEAD shipping the defect it just diagnosed.** Cycle 70 reached the right verdict - agent3 EXCEPTED, with the flake measured three ways - and wrote the repair into the working tree, but the wrap commit was never made. So for a full cycle `.github/workflows/ci.yml` ran the agent3 tree BARE in a push-blocking job while the audit doc and the guard both described a state the gate did not implement. The deliverable of a cycle is a commit, not a correct working tree.

## Older findings - relocated 2026-07-28

R205 / R206 / R207 / R216 / R217 / R217-U1 findings now live in `docs/ORCHESTRATION_PLAN_HISTORY.md`.

**Why, because this WILL recur:** the director context caps this file with
`cap_bytes_head_tail(..., PLAN_CTX_CAP=24000, PLAN_CTX_HEAD=8000)`, so only the
first 8000 and the LAST 16000 bytes reach the director, and
`tests/test_loop_director_context_caps.py::test_real_orchestration_plan_newest_row_survives`
fails when the newest `| R<n> |` row falls outside that tail window. Every cycle
appends a row AND a findings block, but the findings land AFTER the rows - so each
cycle pushes the newest row roughly one findings-block further from EOF while the
row itself barely moves. R216 crossed the line at 16087 bytes from EOF, missing the
window by 87 bytes. **The fix is to relocate old findings blocks, never to shrink the
new row or trim the guard.** Budget about two to three cycles per relocation.

**R217 changed the relocation from a periodic chore into a steady state.** Budgeting
"two to three cycles per relocation" is still a countdown to the same failure; the
distance from the newest row to EOF is just the total size of the findings blocks
sitting after the rows, so the stable rule is to keep exactly ONE findings block at
the tail. R217 relocated R216's block (5509 bytes) at the same time it appended its
own, which put the newest row at about 5000 bytes from EOF instead of 10696. Do the
same every cycle: relocate the previous cycle's findings block verbatim as you append
yours. The newest row then never drifts, and the director keeps seeing the most
recent findings because the block it can read is always the newest one.

## R226 findings - 2026-09-11 (cycle 69, LAND)

**Cycle 68 measured and committed nothing, so the whole deliverable was a dirty
working tree.** This cycle landed it, and the landing was not a rubber stamp:
two read-only adversarial slices in the MAIN checkout (a worktree cannot see
uncommitted work, so worktree fan-out was forbidden) returned findings that
changed what shipped. Slice 1 returned **BLOCK**, slice 2 **SHIP with two
misses**. The merger adjudicated both.

**THE HEADLINE NUMBER, re-derived on the final tree rather than inherited.**
`tools/live_write_tracer.py` over `pytest tests -n 8 --dist loadfile`, all nine
per-process reports merged by the tool's own `--merge`: **13 units across 1
path**, and that path is `moon_sync_inbox/rc_audit_probe_inside.txt` - the one
row the census DECLARES as deliberately unprevented, because the write IS the
assertion in `tests/test_vision_server_http_hardening.py` and the test removes
it again. BEFORE was 201079 units over 17 paths. The AFTER run reproducing
exactly the declared exception and nothing else is the evidence that the other
sixteen rows are closed. Two zero-byte opens survive, both `.lock` files, which
is what a lock is for. `atomic_tmp_units` is empty. Pinned as a JOIN against
the census table at `tests/test_suite_does_not_write_live_tree.py`, not as a
restated literal: flipping the EXCEPTION row to FIXED reds it (observed).

**SLICE 2 FOUND TWO PRODUCERS THE NARROW FIX MISSED, and that is the more
valuable half of the cycle.** The root cause was never "ddragon_mirror_refresh
used `write_text`" - it is that on Windows `Path.write_text` rewrites LF as
CRLF while `read_text` translates it back, and for a TRACKED file that git
normalizes in the index `git status` stays CLEAN, so the defect is invisible
from both directions at once. `tools/daemon_slayer_extract._atomic_write_json`
(writing 44 tracked `data/daemon_slayer/**` JSON files, all pinned `eol=lf`),
its `_atomic_write_text` sibling, and
`tools/daemon_slayer_abilities_extract._atomic_write_json` all had the
identical shape and were latent - they would emit CRLF on the next DS extract.
Fixed, plus a class guard
(`tests/test_tracked_json_producers_emit_lf_bytes.py`, 20 tests) that carries
all six producers including the two RM-287 already fixed, asserts each named
target is still tracked AND still `eol=lf` via `git check-attr` so a rotted
premise fails loudly, and sweeps the tracked tree for CRLF that an earlier
broken run left behind. RED first: 6 real failures across the three unfixed
producers before the fix, 20 passed after.

**SLICE 1'S REFUTATIONS WERE ACCEPTED, NOT ARGUED WITH.** (a) The guard's
docstring claimed "removing any one redirect turns this file red"; it does not.
Narrowing `_tmp_log_path` to pass one filename through leaves both arms of
`_check_logging_handler_ctor` satisfied, and under `--dist loadfile` the test
that would attach that handler need not share the worker. The claim is now four
stated limits with the demonstrated mutation named. (b) The tracer reported
`wrote_bytes` while `_datalen` returns CHARACTERS for text handles - wrong in
the direction that undercounts, by one byte per newline plus UTF-8 expansion.
Renamed `wrote_units` throughout with the unit stated. (c) No merge code
existed for the per-worker reports, and the xdist CONTROLLER wrote an
unsuffixed near-empty file at the documented default path - a partial census
that looks like a clean one. The controller is now suffixed too and
`--merge` ships in the tool. (d) The tracer credited both the `*.tmp` half of
an atomic write and its destination, double-counting; tmp now has its own
bucket that totals never sum. (e) The default report path was inside
`ops/runtime/` - the instrument was a writer in the tree it polices, excluded
from its own count. Moved outside the repo. (f) A raise between patching
`builtins.open` and publishing `_state` would have left the process patched
forever; `_state` is published first. (g) Two tautologies removed, the
tracked-only blind spot declared, one misleading failure message corrected.

**STEP 3 DEFECT-CLASS ENUMERATION.** The prescribed grep over `tests/` returned
**991 instances across 259 files** - a population that must be shown, not
assumed. Layer 1 is lexical and reproducible (`--include=*.py`, receiver
resolved to its binding in the same file): 374 TMP-or-fixture-rooted, 154
local-only with no live marker, 361 with no statically resolvable target
(almost all `self.<attr>` bound in `setUp` from a tmp dir), 82 `.replace(` that
are string operations and not filesystem calls at all, 5 comment-only, and 15
LIVE-PATH candidates - **every one of which was opened by hand and resolves to
a tmp root**, or reads from the live tree and writes to tmp
(`shutil.copyfile(REPO / rel, tmp_path / rel)`, three sites). Layer 2 is the
authoritative one: the instrumented run attributes actual live-tree writes to
nodeids, and it found exactly the declared exception. Sibling trees
cross-checked with the same grep - `agents/agent3_testing/suite` 29,
`tools/tests` 16, `agents/daemon_slayer/tests` 97, 142 together, 3 live-path
candidates, all three safe (two read-live/write-tmp, one `rmtree` of a
tempdir).

**KNOWN AND NOT CLOSED, stated rather than buried.** `lib/ddragon/fetch.py`
now ships its writer as bytes. **CORRECTED 2026-09-11 (R229 / RM-410): the
rest of this sentence self-staled inside its own commit and is struck.** It
read "is still exercised by no test of its own ... carried in the new class
guard's table and nowhere else", which treated a `_PRODUCERS` row as inert
bookkeeping when it is a CALL SITE - the guard this same commit added invokes
every one of its six producers (`_invoke` at
`tests/test_tracked_json_producers_emit_lf_bytes.py:115`, bytes read `:137`,
asserts `:141` and `:146`; `-k fetch` collects 3). The writer HAS behavioural
CRLF coverage and always did. What it genuinely lacked, and what RM-410
shipped, is the byte-pin / tmp / fault-injection half the guard cannot assert -
see `docs/audits/LF_WRITER_DIRECT_COVERAGE_2026-09-11.md`.
`data/meta_build/ddragon/16.18.1/_assets_manifest.json` is UNTRACKED and still
carries 42318 CRLF pairs from a pre-fix run; the tracked bundles were repaired
when the fixed tool re-ran, and the new sweep asserts zero CRLF across all
tracked, git-normalized `.json`/`.txt`. The seven `-text` LFS payloads under
`data/laning_scenarios/` carry CRLF on purpose and are exempt, as
`tests/test_text_line_endings.py` already documents.
